NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS MINUTES 
TUESDAY, September 24, 2013 
 
Present: Sylvia Allen, Supervisor; Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; David Tenney, Supervisor; Jesse 
Thompson, Vice Chairman; and Jonathan M. Nez, Chairman were present in person.  
9:01 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Thompson.  
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: There were no public comments. 
      CONSENT AGENDA : a) Minutes: September 10, 2013 b) Warrant &  Voucher Report over $1000 August 
2013 c) Constable Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, July 2013, August 2013; Winslow Precinct #2, July 2013, 
August 2013; Snowflake Precinct #3, August 2013; Show Low Precinct #5, August 2013 d) Agreement with 
Forest Service f or the continuation of two RAC grant projects for Aripine and Black Canyon Road e) Navajo 
County scoping comments on the Notice of Intent to Prepare an Environmental Impact Statement for the 
Proposed Revision to the Nonessential Experimental Population of the Mexican Wolf (Canis lupus baileyi). 
Federal Register / Vol. 78, No. 150 / 47268 / Monday, August 5, 2013 f) Revised Amendment 6 for contract 
number DE111011001, showing the exhibit as Amendment 6 rather than Amendment 9 : Supervisor Tenney 
made a motion to authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the consent agenda as presented with item f being 
pulled from the agenda; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
EMPLOYEE RECOGNITION: Navajo County Recognizes Joyce Alexander for her excellence in personal 
performance far exceeding organization expectations : Pam Phillips recognized Joyce Ale xander for her 
exceptional service to Navajo County  and honored her with a certificate of recognition, a $50 gift card and a 
leaf to put on the employee recognition tree. 
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of a Statement of Support by Navajo County and the Employee 
Support for the Guard and Reserve (Jack Latham): Item Pulled. 
b. Public Hearing : Consideration and possible approval of Ordinance Number 02-13 Amending the 
Navajo County Predatory Animal Ordinance, Ordinance Number 01 -08, by Amending Article 6 -
Enforcement: Supervisor Sylvia Allen discussed the item. Brandt Clark and Supervisor Tenney made 
comments regarding jurisdiction. Supervisor Tenney suggested to strike the last three words, on private 
land. Supervisor Thompson  made comments about the federal agencies. Chairman Nez opened the 
public hearing. Chairman Nez commented about departments reviewing items that are to be considered 
on the Boards agenda. Chairman Nez closed the public hearing. Supervisor Allen made a motion to 
approve Ordinance Number 02 -13 with a change in 6.3, to remove “on private land ” and put a 
period after livestock ;  motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney . Vote unanimous approving the 
motion.  
c. Report from the County Attorney’s Office on the White Mountain Lakes Recreation District : Brandt 
Clark discussed the item. He indicated  the White Mountain Lakes Recreation District was formed  in 
1993 under the authority of the Board of Supervisors. According to state statute, the Board of 
Supervisors is the governing body of that entity.  When that was created they also created an  advisory 
committee to oversee some of the daily operations  of the recre ation district and to provide 
recommendations to the Board of Supervisors for their approval. Back in May 2006 the White Mountain 
Lakes Recreation District had  created an d amended their rules and regulations  for those individuals

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 09-24-2013 
Page 2 of 5 
 
 
located within t he distric t. In May of 2011 the advisory committee met and  they approved specific 
changes to those May 2006 rules and regulations . However, at that time they did not bring that to the 
Board of Supervisors for their approval and therefore any of the amendments that w ere made in May of 
2011 are not binding. Mr. Clark indicated during his  discussions with the advis ory committee they 
indicated that it was a unanimous vote back in 2011 to approve the rule changes.  Part of the rule 
changes that were passed, were the use of resources of the district without having their assessment being 
current. That rule was not in place in May 2006 and it was approved by the advisory committee in May 
2011. That was th e main subject of the request at  the Aug 27 th public hearing. We found the advisory 
committee has not gotten the approval of the Board of Supervisors, so any of the amendments in May of 
2011 would not be binding at this point.   Supervisor Allen asked about the other changes. Mr. Clark 
stated they approved u pdating rules to comply with state statue and they also cleared up some of the 
language about what kind of floatation  devices are available and can be used . Supervisor Allen then 
inquired what the proper procedure would have been for them to bring it to the B oard of Supervisors 
since we are the governing Board for t he district. Mr. Clark indicated the proper procedures would be  
for the advisory committee to make a recommendation to the Board of Supervisors sitting as the White 
Mountain Lakes Recreation District, hold a public hearing and then after hearing from the public make a 
decision on the recommendation. He indicated  in the past the advisory committee has  brought those  
changes to the Board who approves their budget and expenditures annually. Mr. Clark indicated that his 
recommendation would be to contact the recreation district and let them know that should they choose to 
amend the rules  and regulations and have these binding upon all the members of the recreation  district 
they would need to present them to the Board for their approval. The members are bound by the May of 
2006 rules and regulations which were approved by the Board of Supervisors.  Supervisor Tenney 
asked if the Board of Supervisors could give Mr. Clark direction to draft a letter to the Advisory 
Committee on their behalf. Brandt indicated that he could draft a letter to the chairman of the Advisory 
Committee. Supervisor Tenney requested that the letter indicate that A) that the 2006 rules  were 
approved and B) research has shown that 2006 was approved but 2011 was not and if they want to abide 
by those rules the need to bring those recommendations to the Board. Mr. Clark indicated that he would 
take care of writing the letter.   
d. Consideration and possible action r egarding settlement proposal in Snowflake Power v. Arizona 
Department of Revenue & Navajo County for tax year 2012 . Jason Moore discussed the item and asked 
the Board to go into executive session. At 9:43 a.m. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to go into 
execute session; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
At 10:03 a.m. Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to return to regular  session. Supervisor Tenney 
made comments and then made a motion to take no action at this time; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
      HUMAN RESOURCES : Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions : Kimberly Eavenson  
introduced Gayle Loyd, the new Human Resources Technician. Mrs. Eavenson reviewed the 20 item personnel 
action report. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to approve the personnel action report as presented; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
FINANCE:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of a contract with CBIZ for employee benefit consulting services in 
the amount of $65,000 for phase 1 consulting : Mary Springer  discussed the item. Supervisor Tenney 
made a comment regarding the contract and the health benefits for the employees. Mary Springer 
indicated that the Strategic Planning Team were the ones working on this. Supervisor Whitesinger asked 
about the other company’s prices comparable and the outcomes that we are expecting to receive. Mrs.

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 09-24-2013 
Page 3 of 5 
 
 
Springer indicated that this was not based on price but on qualifications. Homero Vela made comments 
about the company being the best company in comparison. Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to 
approve the contract with CBIZ for employee benefit consulting services in the amount of $65,000 for 
phase 1 consulting; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of a contract with Trinity Correctional Services for inmate food 
services: Joyce Alexander  and Mary Springer  discussed the item . Supervisor Thompso n made a 
motion to approve the contract with Trinity Correctional Services for inmate food services ; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
      WORKFORCE INVESTMENT ACT: Consideration and possible approval of Contract ID number 
DE111011001 Amendment 8 for Program Year 2012 WIA section 503 incentive funds $28,407.00 : James 
Menlove discussed the item. Supervisor Tenney  made a motion  to approve Contract ID number 
DE111011001, Amendment 8 for Program Year 2012 WIA section 503 incentive funds $28,407.00 ; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the item.  
PUBLIC WORKS: 
a. Consideration and possible approval of Amendment Number 1 to the Professional Services Agreement 
with Johnson Walzer Associates, LLC for additional Architectural/Engineering services for the Navajo 
County Public Works Complex for an additional amount of $15,570.00 : Homero Vela discussed the 
item. Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion  to approve Amendment Number 1 to the Professional 
Services Agreement with Johnson Walzer Associates, LLC for additional Architectural/Engineering 
services for the Navajo County Public Works Complex for an additional amount of $15,570.00 ; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of the award of contract number B13 -06-21 Construction Manager-
at-Risk project for Construction of a New Public Works Complex to Haydon Building Corporation : 
Homero Vel a discussed the item. Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to approve the award of 
contract number B13 -06-21 Construction Manager -at-Risk project for Construction of a New Public 
Works Complex to Haydon Building Corporation ; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenn ey. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.  
c. Consideration and possible approval of Task Agreement Number 11 to the Memorandum of Agreement 
between Bureau of Indian Affairs (BIA), Fort Apache Agency and Navajo County for the purchase of 
equipment supplies and  road maintenance materials : Homero Vela discussed the item. Supervisor 
Tenney made a motion to approve Task Agreement Number 11 to the Memorandum of Agreement 
between Bureau of Indian Affairs (BIA), Fort Apache Agency and Navajo County for the purchase of  
equipment supplies and road maintenance materials; motion seconded by Supervisor . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.   
NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT Board of Directors Session : Supervisor Tenney 
made a motion to enter into the Navajo County Flood Control District Board of Directors Session ; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
a. Consideration and possible approval of new members for the Winslow Levee Ad visory Committee :  
Homero Vela presented the item. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve new members for 
the Winslow Levee Advisory Committee; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. 
b. Consideration and possible approval of the expenditure of $15,000 to assist the City of Wi nslow with 
needed maintenance and repairs to the Ruby Wash Diversion Levee : Homero Vela presented the item. 
Supervisor Thompson made a motion  to approve the expenditure of $15,000 to assist the City of

<!-- page 4 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 09-24-2013 
Page 4 of 5 
 
 
Winslow with needed maintenance and repairs to the Ruby Was h Diversion Levee; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
Supervisor Tenney made a motion to return to regular session;  motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 
BOARD OF EQUALIZATION: Supervisor Tenney made a motion to enter into the Board of Equalization; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.   
a. Swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners : The Assessor, Cammy Darris and Appraiser Chris 
Frayer were sworn in by Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board.  
b. Discussion and possible action regarding Petition for Review of Real Prope rty Valuation APN 210 -19-
0630: Cammy Darris presented the item. Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to uphold the assessor’s 
decision; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
c. Discussion and possible action regarding Petition for Review of Real Property Valuation APN 212 -24-
127 and APN 212 -24-128: Cammy Darris presented the item. Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to 
uphold the assessor’s decision; motion seconded by Supervisor Allen. Vote unanimous approving the 
motion. 
d. Discussion and possible action regarding Petition for Review of Real Property Valuation APN 311 -91-
001-026; APN 311 -91-027-046; AP N 311 -91-048-050; APN 311 -91-051-056; APN 311 -91-057-060; 
APN 311 -91-073-077; APN 311 -91-091-093; APN 311 -91-111-116; APN  311-91-221: Chris Frayer  
presented the item  and indicated this included 73 vacant lots . Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to 
uphold the assessor’s decision ; motion seconded by  Supervisor Whitesinger . Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. 
Supervisor Tenney made a motion to return to regular session ; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE : Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Allen  indicated she enjoyed her meetings with Representative Ken Ivory from Utah who made 
presentations in different locations within Navajo and Mojave Counties. She expressed ap preciation to Hunter 
Moore for his assistance . She reported on the LCR RC&D and spoke of a grant they are working on for a 
Cibecue projec t. They will be rehabilitating some of the waterways damage and erosion from the Rodeo 
Chedaski Fire. She reported on the RC&D  conference on the interface bidding and further indicated that she 
made a presentation on new ordinances that could be passed. She was happy to hear of what 4-FRI was doing in 
regards to private property and how we should protect the properties in case of wildfires. She stated she will be 
doing some town hall meetings starting this week and will go into October with the subjects being wolf, the war 
on coal, and the transfer of p ublic lands and any other problems or issues that her constituents would like to 
bring up. She also spoke about a postcard campaign that she started in conjunction with the town hall meetings, 
which will help our federal agencies hear from our private citizens . Supervisor Whitesinger expressed thanks 
to Supervisor Allen for her perspective on to how to better serve the people.  She spoke of the White Mountain 
Apache Tribe events. Supervisor Whitesinger  attended a ribbon cutting at Fatco and spoke about the 
investment that tribe has been impleme nting. She spoke of attending an  environmental impact study scoping 
meeting with the water system project, which encompass three different areas within the Tribe . The project is in 
three parts; building the Minor Flats Dam, a treatment center for the Tribe and a pipeline that goes from Minor 
Flats Dam into Cibecue. The study should take a couple of years and will be about three years before they start 
the project.  She had the chance to participate in the McNary Community meeting and their conc erns and 
challenge in trying to revitalize land into its natural state. Students have been out taking pictures of the area to 
show erosion and other effects in the area. She attended the Sunrise Board meeting and the search f or their 
general manager has been narrowed do wn to two potential applicants . Supervisor Tenney introduced Houston

<!-- page 5 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 09-24-2013 
Page 5 of 5 
 
 
Tenney his son whom was hi red by the Sheriff’s Office and will be attending the police academy in Tucson.  
Supervisor Tenney gave an update about the CSA meeting  where discussion of the upcoming summit  and the 
upcoming legislative session . Supervisor Tenney will be attending the WIR Conference in Grand Junction, 
Colorado and an upcoming meeting with Senate President Andy Biggs and Mr. D avid Farnsworth, who was 
appointed to the Senate in place of Rich Crandall.  Supervisor Tenney big announcement from 4FRI which has 
been transferred from Pioneer Associates to Good Earth Power, Forest Service has task orders which will now 
be issued. Supervisor Tenney also commended the team that he coaches and the recent wins that they have had. 
Supervisor Thompson reported attending the 9/11 event in Winslow and spoke about staying united as cultures 
and coming together.  Supervisor Thompson participated in the South Western Navajo Nation fair in Dilk on 
along with Sheriff Clark, Judge Ralph Hatch and Supervisor Nez . Road damages  with Hopi and Navajo 
Nations working with BIA, N avajo Nation, Hopi Tribe and in working on repairs and  road maintenance. An 
agreement with BIA has been worke d out for transportation on maintenance on three g raders in located in 
District I, District II and Hopi and utilizing personnel, equipment and roads. Chairman Nez spoke of meetings 
at CSA, the Navajo County fair and the South West Navajo Fair and thanked everyone that was involved in the 
planning and volunteering. District one is working with the Navajo Nation and the budget. The Navajo Nation 
has opened the newest casino at Twin Arrows and the gaming compact the tribe has the decision to give funds 
to local sub divisions and governments. Chairman Nez noted that the tribe will be putting a proposal together 
to distribute the funds from gaming revenue. Homero Vela indicated tha t Mr. Jayne is in Boston  at the 
International City County Managers Association Conference and sends his regards. Mr. Vela, Mr. Jayne and Dr. 
Kartchner met with Summit Health officials to discuss how a partnership could be formed to  improve medical 
benefits for Navajo County employees and their dependents . As part of the continued improvement of benefits, 
CBIZ will be doing visits with the county to see how they can better benefit employees  and their dependents .  
James Jayne and Hunter Moore gave a presentation on  ‘state of the county’ on everything from revenue trends 
to upcoming economi c opportunities to the White Mountain Business Owners Roundtable .  Supervisor 
Thompson and Mr. Vela met with the Chinle BIA agency  to provide an operator for the grader for road repairs 
every other week. Mr. Vela also commented that they did stop at the So uth West Region Fair which was very 
well attended. Melissa Buckley advised that the Navajo County Leadership Academy graduation will be on 
Wednesday October 2nd at Northland Pioneer College campus in Snowflake.  
      ADJOURN: At 10:52 a.m. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by  
Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
 
 
 
APPROVED: 
 
Jesse Thompson, Vice Chairman 
 
ATTEST: 
 
 
 
 
DATE:  October 22, 2013 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board
